```
time               action        cellIndividualOffset   reason recorded by MRO
2026-11-10 03:00   MRO change    dB0 -> dB1             HO.IntraSys.TooLate 38 per day
2026-11-11 03:10   MRO change    dB1 -> dB2             HO.IntraSys.TooLate 21 per day
2026-11-11 15:40   MRO change    dB2 -> dB3             HO.IntraSys.TooLate 15 per day
2026-11-13 03:00   MRO change    dB3 -> dB4             HO.IntraSys.TooLate 9 per day
2026-11-15 03:00   MRO change    dB4 -> dB2             HO.IntraSys.TooEarly 26 per day
(end of log)
```

**091.** Which log entries violate the OAM control parameters?

1. Only the 2026-11-13 entry.
2. Only the 2026-11-11 15:40 entry.
3. Only the 2026-11-15 entry, because it reverses the direction.
4. 11-11 15:40 and 11-13 violate the interval and offset limits.

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　4

**詳解**　Intervals: 11-10 03:00 to 11-11 03:10 = 24 h 10 min (allowed); 11-11 03:10 to 15:40 = 12 h 30 min (violation); 11-11 15:40 to 11-13 03:00 = 35 h 20 min (allowed); 11-13 to 11-15 = 48 h (allowed). Deviation from dB0: 1, 2, 3, 4, 2 dB, so only dB4 exceeds 3 dB. Options 1 and 2 each find only one of the two violations. Option 3 is wrong because reversing direction is allowed; dB2 is within limits and 48 h after the previous change.

**出處**　3GPP TS 38.300 V18.11.0 clause 15.5.2.5 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/38_series/38.300/)

</details>

**092.** According to TS 38.300 clause 15.5.2.5, which control parameters shall OAM provide for MRO?

1. None: MRO may change handover parameters freely, and OAM is only informed.
2. The a3-Offset range of the UE, because MRO cannot exceed the UE capability.
3. Trigger-change size and spacing limits, plus configurable Tstore_UE_cntxt.
4. The T310 timer value, which MRO adjusts instead of handover parameters.

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　3

**詳解**　Clause 15.5.2.5: all automatic changes of handover and/or reselection parameters shall be within the ranges allowed by OAM; OAM shall provide the maximum deviation of Handover Trigger and the minimum time between Handover Trigger changes, and Tstore_UE_cntxt shall be configurable. Option 1 contradicts "shall be within the ranges allowed by OAM". Option 2 confuses an ASN.1 range with an MRO control parameter. Option 4 is not in the clause.

**出處**　3GPP TS 38.300 V18.11.0 clause 15.5.2.5 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/38_series/38.300/)

</details>

**093.** How much time passed between the 2026-11-11 03:10 and 2026-11-11 15:40 changes?

1. 12 h 30 min
2. 36 h 40 min
3. 24 h 10 min
4. 11 h 30 min

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　1

**詳解**　15:40 - 03:10 = 12 h 30 min. Option 2 is not any interval in the log. Option 3 is the interval between the first two changes (11-10 03:00 to 11-11 03:10). Option 4 is a subtraction error.

**出處**　3GPP TS 38.300 V18.11.0 clause 15.5.2.5 (minimum time between Handover Trigger changes) (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/38_series/38.300/)

</details>

**094.** What is the purpose of the parameter "Minimum time between Handover Trigger changes"?

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　It defines the minimum allowed interval between two Handover Trigger changes performed by MRO and is used to control the stability and convergence of the algorithm.

**詳解**　TS 38.300 clause 15.5.2.5 gives this purpose. Too-frequent changes would not leave enough time to observe the effect of the previous change, as on 11-11 here.

**出處**　3GPP TS 38.300 V18.11.0 clause 15.5.2.5 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/38_series/38.300/)

</details>

**095.** Did the change back to dB2 on 2026-11-15 reduce the too early handovers?

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　Cannot be determined: the log ends at 2026-11-15 03:00; HO.IntraSys.TooEarly and HO.IntraSys.TooLate for REL-806 after that time are needed.

**詳解**　The log records only the reason for each change, not the outcome afterwards.

**出處**　3GPP TS 28.552 V18.12.0 clause 5.1.1.25.1 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/28_series/28.552/)

</details>

**情境 020**　Grouped RLF reports: mobility or coverage?

Telco-X (fictional) grouped the RLF reports fetched for failures in cell HUA-0402-1 during one week. Tstore_UE_cntxt is 5 s (fictional operator setting).

```
RLF reports with failedPCellId = HUA-0402-1, 2026-11-17 .. 11-23, 214 reports
Group  last handover (previousPCellId, timeConnFailure)      re-establishment / reconnection                      count  median serving SS-RSRP  best neighbour (median SS-RSRP)
G1     none (both absent)                                    reestablishmentCellId = HUA-0402-1                    96     -124 dBm               none reported
G2     none (both absent)                                    re-establishment failed; reconnectCellId = HUA-0402-1 41     -126 dBm               none reported
G3     none (both absent)                                    reestablishmentCellId = HUA-0403-2                    52     -118 dBm               HUA-0403-2 (-103 dBm)
G4     from HUA-0399-1, timeConnFailure 8..19 (0.8-1.9 s)   reestablishmentCellId = HUA-0399-1                    25     -116 dBm               HUA-0399-1 (-108 dBm)
locationInfo: present in 160 of 214 reports (not shown in this summary)
```

**096.** What share of the 214 reports are intra-system mobility failures in the TS 38.300 sense?

1. 44.9%
2. 24.3%
3. 36.0%
4. 64.0%

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　3

**詳解**　G3 is too late (no recent handover, re-establishment in a different cell): 52. G4 is too early (recent handover from HUA-0399-1, 0.8 to 1.9 s < 5 s, re-establishment in the source): 25. Share = (52 + 25) / 214 = 77 / 214 = 0.3598, about 36.0%. G1 and G2 return to the same cell and are not mobility failures. Option 1 is G1 only (96 / 214 = 0.4486). Option 2 is G3 only (52 / 214 = 0.2430). Option 4 is G1 + G2 (137 / 214 = 0.6402).

**出處**　3GPP TS 38.300 V18.11.0 clause 15.5.2.2.2 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/38_series/38.300/)

</details>

**097.** For group G4, whose mobility configuration should be investigated first?

1. The HUA-0402-1 configuration, since the failures were detected there.
2. The configuration at HUA-0399-1, the source of G4's early handovers.
3. The HUA-0403-2 configuration, since it is G3's strongest neighbour.
4. The AMF configuration, since the AMF handles handover preparation.

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　2

**詳解**　In too early handover the trigger was in the source cell's mobility configuration (HUA-0399-1 handed the UEs over too early); TS 38.300 says the node receiving the failure indication may inform the node controlling the cell where the mobility configuration caused the failure via the Handover Report procedure over Xn or the Uplink RAN Configuration Transfer over NG. Option 1 confuses where the RLF occurred with where the decision was made. Option 3 concerns G3. Option 4 is wrong because handover decisions are made by the RAN.

**出處**　3GPP TS 38.300 V18.11.0 clause 15.5.2.2.2 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/38_series/38.300/)

</details>

**098.** According to TS 38.300 clause 15.5.2.1, what does MRO provide besides detecting mobility problems?

1. Automatic correction of coverage holes by changing antenna tilt.
2. A standard definition of intra-NR ping-pong.
3. A replacement for drive tests and MDT.
4. Distinguish mobility, coverage, and other problems.

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　4

**詳解**　Clause 15.5.2.1: "MRO provides means to distinguish the above problems from NR coverage related problems and other problems, not related to mobility." Option 1 is coverage optimisation, not MRO. Option 2 is wrong because only inter-system ping-pong is defined. Option 3 is not stated anywhere in the clause.

**出處**　3GPP TS 38.300 V18.11.0 clause 15.5.2.1 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/38_series/38.300/)

</details>

**099.** How should groups G1 and G2 be interpreted?

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　As a probable coverage problem of HUA-0402-1 rather than a handover-parameter problem: 137 failures with a very weak serving cell, no neighbour reported and recovery in the same cell (to be handled under coverage, 6.1.1).

**詳解**　Without a recent handover and without re-establishment in another cell, none of the three intra-system MRO classes applies; changing handover offsets would not help when no neighbour is available.

**出處**　3GPP TS 38.300 V18.11.0 clauses 15.5.2.1 and 15.5.2.2.2 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/38_series/38.300/); Curriculum 6.1.1

</details>

**100.** Where geographically are the G1 and G2 failures located?

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　Cannot be determined from this summary: the per-report locationInfo (available in 160 of 214 reports) or MDT data must be extracted and mapped.

**詳解**　The summary gives medians and counts only; the location field exists in many reports but is not shown.

**出處**　3GPP TS 38.331 V18.11.0 clause 6.2.2 (RLF-Report-r16, locationInfo-r16) (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/38_series/38.331/); Curriculum 6.2.3 (collecting MDT data)

</details>

**1.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**10.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**14.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**18.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**22.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**30.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**39.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**43.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**47.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**51.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**55.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**59.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**68.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**72.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**81.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**90.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**94.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**98.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**103.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**112.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.
